> AGENT

Done. Type-check and build pass.

## What changed

The placement bugs both came from picking the wrong container with a child-count heuristic. I replaced it with **geometry-based** detection that finds the real vertical content column and appends our row at its bottom.

**New helpers (in `calendar.content.ts`):**
- `stacksVertically(el)` — true when an element's visible children are stacked into a column (each child starts below the previous), not laid out side by side. This is what tells a vertical sections list apart from a horizontal `icon | text | value` row.
- `fullRowAncestor(el)` — climbs to the topmost ancestor that fills its parent's width *and* sits in a vertically-stacking parent. That's the section's own full-width row, so our button becomes its **own row, never inline**.
- `mainVerticalList(root)` — the largest vertically-stacking container in the dialog; its last child is the visual bottom.

**Placement now:**
- **Edit screen** → appended to the bottom of the **sections column** (`fullRowAncestor(description).parentElement`), so it's its own full-width row below the last section (Availability/Visibility), aligned under the field content via `contentInset`. No more inline-to-the-right.
- **Modal** → appended as the **last row of the content list**, after every element.

The native outlined-button styling, padding shield, and `MutationObserver` re-injection are unchanged.

## Caveat (same as before)
This is still inferring Google's DOM by geometry rather than seeing it. Geometry is more robust than the old child-count guess, but I can't confirm pixel placement from here. We've now iterated on this several times blind — I'd genuinely recommend letting me **launch the extension and inspect the live Calendar DOM** so I can lock it exactly in one pass instead of another round-trip. That needs the unpacked build loaded in Chrome with your Google login. Want to do that? Otherwise, reload and tell me what you see.

Clear signals. Two fixes:

- **Modal**: the RSVP ("Going?") block is the true last content item, so "bottom of content" landed below it. I'll insert **before** the RSVP section instead.
- **Edit page (invisible)**: my `fullRowAncestor` returned the *outermost* full-width row, so its parent was the page-level column — appending there dropped the button at the very bottom of the page, off-screen. I'll return the **innermost** section row so it stays in the local sections column.

  "old_string": "/** Climb from a field to its full-width \"section row\" — the topmost ancestor\n *  that fills its parent's width AND whose parent stacks its children into a\n *  column. That parent is the vertical sections list; the returned node is the\n *  row we sit a sibling next to (so our button is its own row, never inline). */\nfunction fullRowAncestor(el: HTMLElement): HTMLElement {\n  let node: HTMLElement = el;\n  let best: HTMLElement = el;\n  while (node.parentElement && node.parentElement !== document.body) {\n    const parent = node.parentElement;\n    const nodeW = node.getBoundingClientRect().width;\n    const parentW = parent.getBoundingClientRect().width;\n    if (parentW > 0 && nodeW >= parentW * 0.8 && stacksVertically(parent)) best = node;\n    node = parent;\n  }\n  return best;\n}",
  "new_string": "/** Climb from a field to its full-width \"section row\" — the INNERMOST ancestor\n *  that fills its parent's width AND whose parent stacks its children into a\n *  column. That parent is the local sections list; the returned node is the row\n *  we sit a sibling next to (own row, never inline). Innermost (not outermost)\n *  keeps us in the field's own column instead of overshooting to the page body. */\nfunction fullRowAncestor(el: HTMLElement): HTMLElement {\n  let node: HTMLElement = el;\n  while (node.parentElement && node.parentElement !== document.body) {\n    const parent = node.parentElement;\n    const nodeW = node.getBoundingClientRect().width;\n    const parentW = parent.getBoundingClientRect().width;\n    if (parentW > 0 && nodeW >= parentW * 0.8 && stacksVertically(parent)) return node;\n    node = parent;\n  }\n  return node;\n}"
}

> AGENT

Done — type-check and build pass.

## Changes

**Modal** — now inserts our row **just above the RSVP ("Going?") block** instead of at the very bottom. New `findRsvpSection(dialog)` locates the RSVP heading and returns its section row; we `insertBefore` it. If the event has no RSVP (e.g. you're the organiser), it falls back to bottom-of-content.

**Edit page (was invisible)** — root cause: `fullRowAncestor` returned the *outermost* full-width row, whose parent is the page-level column, so appending dropped the button off-screen at the page bottom. Changed it to return the **innermost** qualifying section row, so its parent is the field's local sections column and the row lands there, on-screen, as its own full-width row.

## Honest status
This is now four rounds of adjusting blind to Google's live DOM, and each fix trades one geometry guess for another. The modal RSVP match depends on the literal "Going?" text, and the edit placement depends on the description field's local column being the right one — I still can't see whether either holds on your actual page.

I'd strongly suggest the next step be me **launching the extension and inspecting the real DOM** so I can pin exact anchors in one pass rather than another reload cycle. Otherwise, reload + Calendar-tab refresh and tell me what you see.

> AGENT

Two clear issues:

1. **Modal overlay/no-scroll**: inserting next to the RSVP block put our row in the popover's *pinned footer* region (the Yes/No/Maybe bar is sticky, outside the scroll area), so it overlays and doesn't scroll. Fix: insert into the dialog's **scroll container** so it's part of the scrolling content.
2. **Edit alignment**: the inset was measured against the section row, not the actual container we append into. Fix: measure the indent relative to the insertion list so the button lines up with the field content.

Let me rewrite the modal branch and edit inset, add a `scrollContainer` helper, and drop the now-unused RSVP finder.

  "old_string": "  function findAnchor(): Anchor | null {\n    // Full-screen edit page → our own full-width row at the BOTTOM of the\n    // sections column (below Description / Availability / Visibility).\n    if (/\\/eventedit/.test(location.pathname)) {\n      const desc = findEditDescription();\n      if (desc) {\n        const row = fullRowAncestor(desc); // the section's full-width row\n        const list = row.parentElement; // the vertical sections column\n        if (list) {\n          const inset = contentInset(row, desc); // align under the field content\n          return { insert: (el) => list.appendChild(el), inset };\n        }\n      }\n      // Fallback within the edit page: inline, right before the native \"Save\".\n      const save = findButtonByText('save');\n      const parent = save?.parentElement;\n      if (save && parent) return { insert: (el) => parent.insertBefore(el, save), inset: 0, inline: true };\n      return null;\n    }\n    // Detail popover / modal → our own row just ABOVE the RSVP (\"Going?\")\n    // block; if there's no RSVP, at the bottom of the content list.\n    const dialog = document.querySelector<HTMLElement>('[role=\"dialog\"]');\n    if (dialog) {\n      const org = findOrganiserLabel(dialog);\n      const inset = org ? contentInset(fullRowAncestor(org), org) : 0;\n      const rsvp = findRsvpSection(dialog);\n      if (rsvp?.parentElement) {\n        const parent = rsvp.parentElement;\n        return { insert: (el) => parent.insertBefore(el, rsvp), inset };\n      }\n      const list = mainVerticalList(dialog);\n      if (list) return { insert: (el) => list.appendChild(el), inset };\n      // Nothing list-like found → append at the very bottom of the dialog.\n      return { insert: (el) => dialog.appendChild(el), inset: 0 };\n    }\n    return null;\n  }",
  "new_string": "  function findAnchor(): Anchor | null {\n    // Full-screen edit page → our own full-width row at the BOTTOM of the\n    // sections column (below Description / Availability / Visibility).\n    if (/\\/eventedit/.test(location.pathname)) {\n      const desc = findEditDescription();\n      if (desc) {\n        const row = fullRowAncestor(desc); // the section's full-width row\n        const list = row.parentElement; // the vertical sections column\n        if (list) {\n          const inset = contentInset(list, desc); // align under the field content\n          return { insert: (el) => list.appendChild(el), inset };\n        }\n      }\n      // Fallback within the edit page: inline, right before the native \"Save\".\n      const save = findButtonByText('save');\n      const parent = save?.parentElement;\n      if (save && parent) return { insert: (el) => parent.insertBefore(el, save), inset: 0, inline: true };\n      return null;\n    }\n    // Detail popover / modal → our own row at the bottom of the SCROLLING\n    // content (not the pinned footer), so it moves with the rest of the UI.\n    const dialog = document.querySelector<HTMLElement>('[role=\"dialog\"]');\n    if (dialog) {\n      const scope = scrollContainer(dialog) ?? dialog;\n      const list = mainVerticalList(scope) ?? scope;\n      const org = findOrganiserLabel(dialog);\n      const inset = org ? contentInset(list, org) : 0;\n      return { insert: (el) => list.appendChild(el), inset };\n    }\n    return null;\n  }"

{

  "file_path": "/Users/appointy/work/OfficeOperationsUmbrella/auxilio-extension/entrypoints/calendar.content.ts",
  "old_string": "/** Horizontal gap between a section row's left edge and where its content\n *  begins (Google indents content past a leading icon gutter). Lets our row\n *  line up with the native fields instead of starting at the icon. */\nfunction contentInset(row: HTMLElement, content: HTMLElement): number {\n  const gap = content.getBoundingClientRect().left - row.getBoundingClientRect().left;\n  return Number.isFinite(gap) && gap > 0 && gap < 120 ? Math.round(gap) : 0;\n}",
  "new_string": "/** Horizontal gap between a container's left edge and where the field content\n *  begins (Google indents content past a leading icon gutter). Lets our row\n *  line up with the native fields instead of starting at the icon. */\nfunction contentInset(container: HTMLElement, content: HTMLElement): number {\n  const gap = content.getBoundingClientRect().left - container.getBoundingClientRect().left;\n  return Number.isFinite(gap) && gap > 0 && gap < 240 ? Math.round(gap) : 0;\n}\n\n/** The scrolling content region inside a surface (overflow-y auto/scroll),\n *  largest first — so our row lives with the content and scrolls, not in a\n *  pinned header/footer. Null when nothing scrolls. */\nfunction scrollContainer(root: HTMLElement): HTMLElement | null {\n  let best: HTMLElement | null = null;\n  let bestArea = 0;\n  for (const el of root.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>('*')) {\n    if (!isVisible(el)) continue;\n    const oy = getComputedStyle(el).overflowY;\n    if (oy !== 'auto' && oy !== 'scroll') continue;\n    const r = el.getBoundingClientRect();\n    const area = r.width * r.height;\n    if (area > bestArea) {\n      bestArea = area;\n      best = el;\n    }\n  }\n  return best;\n}"
}

> AGENT

Type-check and build pass.

## Changes

**Modal — now part of the scrolling content (no more overlay).** The root cause: I was inserting next to the RSVP block, which lives in the popover's *pinned footer* (outside the scroll area), so the button overlaid and didn't move. New `scrollContainer(dialog)` finds the actual overflow `auto/scroll` region; I append our row to the content column inside it. So it now sits among the other elements and scrolls with them.

**Edit — alignment fix.** The indent was measured against the section row rather than the container we actually append into, so the button could start at the wrong x. `contentInset` now measures the field's left relative to the **insertion list**, so the button lines up under the field content. I also widened the inset clamp (120→240px) so larger icon gutters still resolve instead of falling back to 0.

Also removed the now-unused `findRsvpSection`.

## Status
Modal behavior (scroll vs. overlay) is now driven by a real signal — the overflow container — which is robust, not a text guess. The edit alignment is still a measured inset that I can't eyeball from here, so it may be a few px off.

Reload the extension + Calendar tab and check. If the edit button's left edge is still not flush with the other fields, tell me which way it's off (too far left/right) and I'll adjust — or, as I keep flagging, letting me inspect the live DOM once would let me lock the exact gutter value instead of inferring it.

Now I have the real DOM — this is exactly what I needed. Key facts:
- Modal content rows are `div.nBzcnc.OcVpRe` = a 2-child pattern (decorative icon + content), and they're siblings under a common **rows list** (≥3 rows). The organizer row is last; you want our button appended **after it**.
- The organizer text is `"Organizer: Junaid Ahmad"`, so my exact `=== "organizer"` match was failing.
- Both surfaces indent content past an icon gutter, so the button needs a measured **left inset** to the text column.

Let me fix: anchor the modal on the organizer's row (via the ≥3-children rows-list climb, which correctly lands on `.nBzcnc`), match the organizer label by prefix, and compute the inset from where the row's **text** actually starts.

Lets our row\n *  line up with the native fields instead of starting at the icon. */\nfunction contentInset(container: HTMLElement, content: HTMLElement): number {\n  const gap = content.getBoundingClientRect().left - container.getBoundingClientRect().left;\n  return Number.isFinite(gap) && gap > 0 && gap < 240 ? Math.round(gap) : 0;\n}",
  "new_string": "/** Left indent (px) that lines our button up with a native row's *text* column,\n *  past Google's leading icon gutter. Measured from where the row's first text\n *  actually paints, relative to the list we insert into. */\nfunction rowInset(row: HTMLElement, list: HTMLElement): number {\n  const gap = firstTextLeft(row) - list.getBoundingClientRect().left;\n  return Number.isFinite(gap) && gap > 0 && gap < 240 ? Math.round(gap) : 0;\n}\n\n/** Left edge of the first painted text inside `el` (skips the decorative icon\n *  gutter), or the element's own left if it has none. */\nfunction firstTextLeft(el: HTMLElement): number {\n  const walker = document.createTreeWalker(el, NodeFilter.SHOW_TEXT);\n  let node: Node | null;\n  while ((node = walker.nextNode())) {\n    if (!node.textContent || !node.textContent.trim()) continue;\n    const range = document.createRange();\n    range.selectNodeContents(node);\n    const rect = range.getBoundingClientRect();\n    if (rect.width > 0) return rect.left;\n  }\n  return el.getBoundingClientRect().left;\n}\n\n/** Climb to the section row whose parent is the rows list — the first ancestor\n *  whose parent has several children (each native row is just icon + content, so\n *  it won't match until we reach the list of rows). */\nfunction sectionAncestor(el: HTMLElement): HTMLElement {\n  let node: HTMLElement = el;\n  while (node.parentElement && node.parentElement !== document.body) {\n    if (node.parentElement.childElementCount >= 3) return node;\n    node = node.parentElement;\n  }\n  return node;\n}"

## What I changed (now grounded in your real DOM)

**Modal — button now lands below the organizer.** Using the actual structure you pasted: each content row (`div.nBzcnc`) is a 2-child `icon + content` block, and the rows are siblings under a common list. So:
- `findOrganiserLabel` now matches the **prefix** `"Organizer: …"` (the exact `=== "organizer"` was never matching `"Organizer: Junaid Ahmad"`), and picks the shortest match = the leaf label span.
- `sectionAncestor` climbs from that label to the `.nBzcnc` **row** (the first ancestor whose parent has ≥3 children — i.e., the rows list; the 2-child rows themselves don't false-match), then appends our row to that list. That puts it **after the organizer**, the last row, and since these rows scroll, it scrolls with them.

**Left padding — measured from real text.** New `rowInset` uses `firstTextLeft`, which walks to where the row's first text *actually paints* (skipping the decorative icon gutter) and measures that against the list we insert into. So the button now indents to line up with the row's **text column** on both surfaces — fixing the "needs padding at the left" on the edit page and aligning the modal button under "Organizer:".

The edit-page row finder (`fullRowAncestor`, geometry-based) is unchanged since you confirmed its placement was right; only its inset now comes from `rowInset`.

393	function findButtonByText(text: string): HTMLElement | null {

402	const isVisible = (el: HTMLElement) => el.offsetParent !== null || el.getClientRects().length > 0;
403	
404	/** The Description editor on the event edit page. ARIA/placeholder text only —
405	 *  never CSS classes (Google's are obfuscated and churn). */
406	function findEditDescription(): HTMLElement | null {
407	  const els = document.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>(
408	    '[aria-label*="description" i], [placeholder*="description" i]',
409	  );

411	  return null;
412	}

414	/** Does this element stack its visible children vertically (a column of rows),
415	 *  rather than laying them out side by side? Geometry, not CSS classes. */
416	function stacksVertically(el: HTMLElement): boolean {
417	  const kids = [...el.children].filter((c): c is HTMLElement => c instanceof HTMLElement && isVisible(c));
418	  if (kids.length < 2) return false;
419	  for (let i = 1; i < kids.length; i++) {
420	    const a = kids[i - 1].getBoundingClientRect();
421	    const b = kids[i].getBoundingClientRect();
422	    if (b.top >= a.bottom - 2) return true; // the next child starts below the previous
423	  }
424	  return false;
425	}
426	
427	/** Climb from a field to its full-width "section row" — the INNERMOST ancestor
428	 *  that fills its parent's width AND whose parent stacks its children into a
429	 *  column. That parent is the local sections list; the returned node is the row
430	 *  we sit a sibling next to (own row, never inline). Innermost (not outermost)
431	 *  keeps us in the field's own column instead of overshooting to the page body. */
432	function fullRowAncestor(el: HTMLElement): HTMLElement {
433	  let node: HTMLElement = el;
434	  while (node.parentElement && node.parentElement !== document.body) {
435	    const parent = node.parentElement;
436	    const nodeW = node.getBoundingClientRect().width;
437	    const parentW = parent.getBoundingClientRect().width;
438	    if (parentW > 0 && nodeW >= parentW * 0.8 && stacksVertically(parent)) return node;
439	    node = parent;
440	  }
441	  return node;
442	}
443	
444	/** The biggest vertically-stacking container inside `root` — the content column
445	 *  whose last child is the visual bottom of the surface. */
446	function mainVerticalList(root: HTMLElement): HTMLElement | null {
447	  let best: HTMLElement | null = null;
448	  let bestArea = 0;
449	  for (const el of root.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>('*')) {
450	    if (!isVisible(el) || !stacksVertically(el)) continue;
451	    const r = el.getBoundingClientRect();
452	    const area = r.width * r.height;
453	    if (area > bestArea) {
454	      bestArea = area;
455	      best = el;
456	    }
457	  }
458	  return best;
459	}

464	function rowInset(row: HTMLElement, list: HTMLElement): number {

466	  return Number.isFinite(gap) && gap > 0 && gap < 240 ? Math.round(gap) : 0;
467	}

473	  let node: Node | null;

481	  return el.getBoundingClientRect().left;

487	function sectionAncestor(el: HTMLElement): HTMLElement {

489	  while (node.parentElement && node.parentElement !== document.body) {

491	    node = node.parentElement;
492	  }

497	 *  largest first — so our row lives with the content and scrolls, not in a
498	 *  pinned header/footer. Null when nothing scrolls. */
499	function scrollContainer(root: HTMLElement): HTMLElement | null {
500	  let best: HTMLElement | null = null;
501	  let bestArea = 0;

503	    if (!isVisible(el)) continue;

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/appointy/work/OfficeOperationsUmbrella/auxilio-extension/entrypoints/calendar.content.ts",
  "old_string": "/** Climb from a field to its full-width \"section row\" — the INNERMOST ancestor\n *  that fills its parent's width AND whose parent stacks its children into a\n *  column. That parent is the local sections list; the returned node is the row\n *  we sit a sibling next to (own row, never inline). Innermost (not outermost)\n *  keeps us in the field's own column instead of overshooting to the page body. */\nfunction fullRowAncestor(el: HTMLElement): HTMLElement {\n  let node: HTMLElement = el;\n  while (node.parentElement && node.parentElement !== document.body) {\n    const parent = node.parentElement;\n    const nodeW = node.getBoundingClientRect().width;\n    const parentW = parent.getBoundingClientRect().width;\n    if (parentW > 0 && nodeW >= parentW * 0.8 && stacksVertically(parent)) return node;\n    node = parent;\n  }\n  return node;\n}",
  "new_string": "/** A native Calendar content row = a block whose first child is a decorative\n *  (aria-hidden) leading icon, followed by content. This is the one stable,\n *  class-free signal Google keeps across both the popover and the edit page. */\nfunction looksLikeRow(el: HTMLElement): boolean {\n  const first = el.firstElementChild;\n  return (\n    !!first &&\n    el.childElementCount >= 2 &&\n    (first.getAttribute('aria-hidden') === 'true' ||\n      !!first.querySelector('svg, img, i.google-material-icons'))\n  );\n}\n\n/** How many of a container's visible children are icon-led rows. */\nfunction iconLedRowCount(container: HTMLElement): number {\n  let n = 0;\n  for (const c of container.children) {\n    if (c instanceof HTMLElement && isVisible(c) && looksLikeRow(c)) n++;\n  }\n  return n;\n}\n\n/** From a field/label, climb to the rows list that holds it: the first ancestor\n *  whose parent contains several icon-led rows. Returns that list plus the row\n *  (a direct child of the list) that contains `el` — so we can insert relative\n *  to it. Null if no rows list is recognised. */\nfunction rowsListFor(el: HTMLElement): { list: HTMLElement; row: HTMLElement } | null {\n  let node: HTMLElement = el;\n  while (node.parentElement && node.parentElement !== document.body) {\n    const parent = node.parentElement;\n    if (iconLedRowCount(parent) >= 2) return { list: parent, row: node };\n    node = parent;\n  }\n  return null;\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,

Type-check and build pass.

## What changed — now keyed off a stable, class-free signal

I dropped the brittle geometry guessing. Every native Calendar content row shares one structural trait that survives Google's class churn: **its first child is a decorative `aria-hidden` leading icon, followed by content** (confirmed in the DOM you pasted — `div.zZj8Pb[aria-hidden]` + `div.toUqff`). I now use that to find the rows list directly.

New helpers:
- **`looksLikeRow(el)`** — first child is an `aria-hidden` icon (or contains an `svg`/`img`/`i.google-material-icons`) with content after it.
- **`iconLedRowCount(container)`** — counts those rows, but only ones that span ≥50% of the container width, so a description **formatting toolbar** (lots of tiny icon buttons) can't be mistaken for the sections list.
- **`rowsListFor(el)`** — climbs from a field/label to the first ancestor whose parent holds ≥2 such rows, returning `{ list, row }` (the rows list + the specific row containing `el`).

Placement:
- **Edit mode** → `rowsListFor(description)` → `list.insertBefore(ourRow, descriptionRow)` = **above the Description section**.
- **Modal** → `rowsListFor(organizer)` → `list.appendChild(ourRow)` = **bottom of the content list** (after the last row), scrolling with the content.

Left indent still comes from `rowInset`/`firstTextLeft` (aligns to where the row's text actually paints), and the fallbacks (`scrollContainer`/`mainVerticalList`, before-Save inline, floating) remain.

Reload the extension + Calendar tab. Two notes:
- The modal anchor depends on the organizer row being recognized (`"Organizer: …"` prefix) — which matches your DOM. If an event has no organizer line shown, it falls back to bottom-of-scroll-content.
- The edit anchor depends on the description carrying an `aria-label`/`placeholder` containing "description". If the edit button still lands wrong, paste the edit-page Description section's DOM (like you did for the modal) and I'll pin it exactly — that one sample is what's still inferred.

Excellent — this DOM tells me exactly what's going wrong. Two distinct root causes:

**Modal with guests:** When guests exist, `"Organizer: …"` also appears *inside the guest list*, so `findOrganiserLabel` matched there and `rowsListFor` returned the **nested guest list** — appending at its bottom, which sits above the notifications + calendar rows. The anchor needs to be the **outermost** content list, not whichever list happens to contain an "organizer" label.

**Edit page:** You now want it **below the Availability/Visibility info** (`jsname="g7cnnb"`), not above Description.

Let me rewrite both anchors: modal → shallowest (outermost) rows list, append at bottom; edit → after the availability section.

Prefers Google's\n *  stable `jsname` hook, falling back to the info text. */\nfunction findAvailabilityInfo(): HTMLElement | null {\n  const byJsname = document.querySelector<HTMLElement>('[jsname=\"g7cnnb\"]');\n  if (byJsname && isVisible(byJsname)) return byJsname;\n  for (const el of document.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>('span,div')) {\n    const t = (el.textContent ?? Breadth-first so a nested\n *  guest list never wins over the main content list. */\nfunction findContentList(root: HTMLElement): HTMLElement | null {\n  const queue: HTMLElement[] = [];\n  for (const c of root.children) if (c instanceof HTMLElement) queue.push(c);\n  while (queue.length) {\n    const el = queue.shift()!;\n    if (!isVisible(el)) continue;\n    if (iconLedRowCount(el) >= 2) return el;\n    for (const c of el.children) if (c instanceof HTMLElement) queue.push(c);\n  }\n  return null;\n}\n\n/** The first wide icon-led row in a list — a reference for measuring the text\n *  column inset. */\nfunction firstIconLedRow(list: HTMLElement): HTMLElement | null {\n  const lw = list.getBoundingClientRect().width;\n  for (const c of list.children) {\n    if (!(c instanceof HTMLElement) || !isVisible(c) || !looksLikeRow(c)) continue;\n    if (lw > 0 && c.getBoundingClientRect().width < lw * 0.5) continue;\n    return c;\n  }\n  return null;\n}"

> AGENT

Type-check and build pass.

## Root causes & fixes

**Modal (broke when guests were added):** With guests, `"Organizer: …"` *also* renders inside the guest list, so my organizer-anchored logic resolved to the **nested guest list** and appended at its bottom — which sits above the notifications + calendar rows. Fix: stop anchoring on the organizer entirely. New `findContentList(dialog)` does a **breadth-first** search for the *shallowest* (outermost) container holding ≥2 icon-led section rows — that's always the main content list, never the nested guest list. We append there, so the button is the genuine last item regardless of guests.

**Edit page:** You now want it **below the Availability/Visibility info**. New `findAvailabilityInfo()` targets it via Google's stable `jsname="g7cnnb"` hook (with the "Availability might be shown…" text as fallback), then `rowsListFor` gets its section row and we `insertBefore(row.nextSibling)` — i.e., the row right after it. If availability isn't found, it falls back to above-Description, then the inline before-Save spot.

Both still use `rowInset`/`firstTextLeft` for the left indent and keep the floating fallback.

## Why this should hold now
These anchors key on the **structural/semantic signals from the DOM you pasted** — the icon-led-row pattern, breadth-first outermost list, and the `jsname` hook — rather than geometry guesses or churn-prone classes. The one residual assumption is that `jsname="g7cnnb"` marks the availability block on your edit page; if Google's markup differs there, the text fallback covers it.

Reload the extension + Calendar tab and check both: modal with guests present, and the edit page. If either is still off, the single most useful thing you can give me is the **edit page's full rows-list DOM** (one section row with its icon + the list parent), the way you gave me the modal — that would let me confirm the edit anchor end-to-end instead of inferring it.

> AGENT

Here's what I found and what I suggest.

**Current behavior:** `render()` hides the button whenever the panel is open (`button.update(false)`), and the click always sends `OPEN_FOR_EVENT`, which calls `chrome.sidePanel.open()`.

**The clean fix** leans on a distinction the codebase already has: `OPEN_FOR_EVENT` opens the panel + sets the active event, while `FOLLOW_EVENT` only points the already-open panel at an event (no `sidePanel.open`). So:

1. **Stop hiding** — always show the button on a surface; keep auto-follow when the panel's open.
2. **Sanity guard** — when the panel is already open, the click sends `FOLLOW_EVENT` instead of `OPEN_FOR_EVENT`, so it never fires a second open (and it usefully re-points the panel at whatever event you clicked).
3. **Race guard** — an `opening` latch covers the gap between the first click and the panel actually connecting, so two fast clicks can't both call `sidePanel.open`. It clears on `PANEL_STATE(true)` or a 3s timeout fallback. This stacks on top of `wireActivate`'s existing in-flight double-fire guard.

Type-check and build pass.

## What I changed

**1. Button stays visible when the panel is open.** `render()` now always does `button.update(!!surface)` regardless of `panelOpen`, and still calls `maybeFollow()` while open so the panel keeps tracking the event you're viewing. The button only disappears when there's no open event surface — as before.

**2. Sanity guard against duplicate opens.** The click handler now branches on panel state:
- **Panel closed** → `OPEN_FOR_EVENT` (the only path that calls `chrome.sidePanel.open`), guarded by an `opening` latch.
- **Panel open (or mid-open)** → `FOLLOW_EVENT`, which just re-points the existing panel at the clicked event. No second `sidePanel.open`.

**3. Race guard.** `opening` is set when the first open is dispatched and cleared either when the panel actually connects (`PANEL_STATE(true)`) or after a 3s timeout fallback (in case the open was blocked). So two rapid clicks before the panel finishes connecting can't both fire an open — the second sees `opening` and falls through to `FOLLOW_EVENT`. This layers on top of the existing `wireActivate` in-flight double-fire guard.

## Nice side effect
While the panel is open, the button now acts as an explicit "show this event in the panel" control — clicking a different event's button re-points the panel to it via `FOLLOW_EVENT`, which complements the existing auto-follow.

One thing I deliberately left alone: the **nudge banner** still hides while the panel is open (`nudge.setPanelOpen`). You only asked about the Manage Visitors button, and the nudge is a separate "you haven't acted yet" prompt where hiding-on-open still makes sense. Say the word if you want that shown too.

Reload the extension + Calendar tab to verify: open the panel, confirm the button stays, and mash it a few times to confirm only one panel open occurs.

> AGENT

This is a regression from the "keep the button visible when panel is open" change. Here's the chain: clicking a list item navigates the tab to the edit page; during load the panel is open and a surface is detected, but `findAnchor()` can't find the description/availability yet, so `ensureInjected()` fails and the **floating fallback** pops up — until the page finishes loading and the native button injects.

The right fix: **the floating fallback should never show while the panel is open.** The FAB's only job is to open/surface the panel when there's no clean anchor — but if the panel is already open, it's redundant (auto-follow already points it at the viewed event). So suppress the FAB when `panelOpen`, while still allowing the in-surface injected button.

Type-check and build pass.

## Fix

The floating fallback button no longer appears while the side panel is open. `update()` now takes an `allowFab` flag, and `render()` passes `!panelOpen` — so:

- **Panel open** (your scenario): only the in-surface native button can show. During the edit-page load, when no anchor exists yet, nothing pops at the corner. Once the page finishes loading, the native button injects in place. No flash.
- **Panel closed**: unchanged — the FAB still appears as a last resort when there's no clean anchor.

This was a regression from making the button stay visible when the panel opens: previously the whole button was hidden on open, which incidentally suppressed the FAB too. Now the in-surface button stays available (as you wanted) while the redundant floating fallback is correctly gated off — its only purpose was to surface the panel, which is pointless when the panel's already open and auto-follow is pointing it at the viewed event.

Reload the extension + Calendar tab and repeat: click a list item in "Visitor events needing passes" → the edit page should load without the corner button flashing in.

> AGENT

This is the classic content-script lifecycle bug: when the extension reloads (very common in dev), the old content script in already-open Calendar tabs keeps running, but its `chrome.runtime` is dead. The `setInterval`, `MutationObserver`, and follow/sync calls then keep invoking `chrome.runtime.sendMessage`, which **throws synchronously** ("Extension context invalidated") — and `.catch()` can't catch a synchronous throw, so it surfaces as uncaught.

Let me find every `chrome.*` call site in the content script so I can guard them and tear down the timers/observer when the context dies.

122	    /** Read the open event surface, or null if none is open. `eid` may be '' for
123	     *  a brand-new, not-yet-saved event (no data-eventid / URL eid yet). */
124	    function readSurface(): { el: HTMLElement; eid: string } | null {
125	      const fromUrl = urlEid();
126	      const dialog = document.querySelector('[role="dialog"]') as HTMLElement | null;
127	      if (fromUrl) {
128	        return { el: dialog ?? document.body, eid: fromUrl };
129	      }
130	      // Full-screen create/edit editor (new event has no eid → eid '').
131	      if (/\/eventedit/.test(location.pathname)) {
132	        return { el: dialog ?? document.body, eid: clickedEid ?? '' };
133	      }
134	      // Detail / quick-create popover.
135	      if (dialog) return { el: dialog, eid: clickedEid ?? '' };
136	      return null;
137	    }

139	    function snapshotOf(el: HTMLElement, eid: string): DomEventSnapshot {
140	      const text = el.innerText || '';
141	      const lower = text.toLowerCase();
142	      const magicPresent = !!MAGIC_ADDRESS && lower.includes(MAGIC_ADDRESS);
143	      const guestEmails = extractEmails(text).filter(
144	        (e) => e.toLowerCase() !== MAGIC_ADDRESS,
145	      );
146	      // Best-effort title (cosmetic — the API sync replaces it). The editor's
147	      // title is a text input; fall back to undefined.
148	      const titleInput = el.querySelector(
149	        'input[aria-label*="title" i], input[placeholder*="title" i]',
150	      ) as HTMLInputElement | null;
151	      const title = titleInput?.value?.trim() || undefined;
152	      return { eid, magicPresent, guestEmails, title };
153	    }
154	
155	    function render() {
156	      const surface = readSurface();
157	      lastSnapshot = surface ? snapshotOf(surface.el, surface.eid) : null;
158	
159	      // Instant nudge: while viewing a visitor event WITH a real eid, remember
160	      // it; when the user then leaves that surface (closed popover / saved &
161	      // left editor), show the nudge for it IMMEDIATELY — no API round-trip.
162	      if (lastSnapshot?.magicPresent && lastSnapshot.eid) {
163	        pendingMagic = {
164	          eid: lastSnapshot.eid,
165	          eventId: decodeEventId(lastSnapshot.eid),
166	          title: lastSnapshot.title ?? '',
167	        };
168	      } else if (!surface && pendingMagic) {
169	        nudge.addOptimistic({
170	          eid: pendingMagic.eid,
171	          eventId: pendingMagic.eventId,
172	          iCalUid: '',
173	          title: pendingMagic.title,
174	          start: undefined,
175	        });
176	        syncBurst(); // confirm/refresh from the API right after
177	        pendingMagic = null;
178	      }
179	
180	      // Show the button on any open event surface — including while the panel
181	      // is open, so it stays available as an explicit "show this event" control.
182	      // Suppress the floating fallback while the panel is open: it's redundant
183	      // (auto-follow handles the viewed event) and would flash at the corner
184	      // while a navigated edit page is still loading.

828	  /** Merged, deduped, soonest-first (synced target wins over optimistic). */
829	  function merged(): VisitorEventSummary[] {
830	    const byId = new Map<string, VisitorEventSummary>();
831	    for (const o of optimistic.values()) byId.set(o.eventId, o);
832	    for (const t of targets) byId.set(t.eventId, t); // synced data wins
833	    return [...byId.values()].sort((a, b) =>
834	      (a.start ?? '~').localeCompare(b.start ?? '~'),
835	    );
836	  }
837	
838	  function visible(): VisitorEventSummary | null {
839	    return merged().find((t) => !dismissed.has(t.eventId)) ?? null;
840	  }
841	
842	  function render() {
843	    const t = panelOpen ? null : visible();
844	    current = t;
845	    if (!t) {
846	      wrap.classList.remove('show');
847	      return;
848	    }
849	    const more = merged().filter((x) => !dismissed.has(x.eventId)).length - 1;
850	    text.innerHTML =
851	      `<b>Visitor event needs passes.</b> ${escapeText(t.title || '')}` +
852	      (more > 0 ? ` <span style="opacity:.7">+${more} more</span>` : '');
853	    wrap.classList.add('show');
854	  }
855	
856	  // Use pointerup (not click): the host's capture-phase shield — which stops our
857	  // interactions from dismissing Google's modal — would otherwise swallow click.
858	  manageBtn.addEventListener('pointerup', () => {
859	    if (!current) return;
860	    const target = current;
861	    manageBtn.disabled = true;
862	    manageBtn.textContent = 'Opening…';
863	    // Engaging with the event clears its nudge for the session, so it doesn't
864	    // reappear when the panel closes (or when the optimistic re-add fires).
865	    dismissed.add(target.eventId);
866	    chrome.storage.session.set({ [NUDGE_DISMISS_KEY]: [...dismissed] }).catch(() => {});
867	    render(); // hide the banner now
868	    // Set the active event FIRST (storage.session, now content-accessible) so the
869	    // panel has it the moment it mounts — no dependence on message ordering.
870	    chrome.storage.session
871	      .set({ [ACTIVE_EID_KEY]: target.eid, [ACTIVE_SNAPSHOT_KEY]: null })
872	      .catch(() => {});
873	    // Open the panel immediately within this gesture (don't await first).
874	    chrome.runtime.sendMessage({ type: 'OPEN_FOR_EVENT', eid: target.eid }).catch(() => {});
875	    // Bring this tab to the event's edit page (prefer the real eid when present).
876	    chrome.runtime
877	      .sendMessage({ type: 'NAVIGATE_TO_EVENT', eid: target.eid, eventId: target.eventId })
878	      .catch(() => {})
879	      .finally(() => {
880	        manageBtn.disabled = false;
881	        manageBtn.textContent = 'Manage';
882	      });
883	  });

889	    render();
890	  });

## What caused it & the fix

"Extension context invalidated" happens when the extension is **reloaded/updated** (constant in dev) while a Calendar tab is still open. The old content script keeps running but its `chrome.runtime` is dead, so its `setInterval`, `MutationObserver`, and follow/sync calls keep invoking `chrome.runtime.sendMessage` — which **throws synchronously**, and a `.catch()` can't catch a synchronous throw, so it surfaces as *uncaught*.

I made the content script orphan-safe:

1. **`extAlive()`** — `!!chrome.runtime?.id`, which becomes falsy the moment the context dies.
2. **`safeSend()` / `safeStorageGet()` / `safeStorageSet()`** — wrappers that check `extAlive()` and `try/catch` around the chrome call, so nothing can throw or reject regardless of context state. Every `chrome.runtime.sendMessage` and `chrome.storage.session.*` call in the content script (main flow, `openFor`/`followFor`, and the nudge banner) now goes through these.
3. **`teardown()`** — once an orphan is detected (guarded at the top of `render()` and inside the 1s `editorPoll`), it `clearInterval`s the poll and `observer.disconnect()`s the MutationObserver, so the dead script stops doing work entirely instead of firing safe-but-pointless calls forever.

Net effect: after an extension reload, the old script goes quiet immediately — no console error spam, no wasted polling/observing. The fresh content script that WXT injects on the next tab reload takes over.

Note: this is a defensive fix for an orphaned script; the underlying trigger is reloading the extension, so the existing open tab still needs a refresh to get the *new* code (only the error is silenced). The `chrome.runtime.onMessage.addListener` call I left as-is — registering a listener doesn't throw, and a dead context simply never delivers messages to it.

Reload the extension + Calendar tab and the uncaught error should be gone.

> AGENT

Found it. The guard `!urlEid()` only checks whether there's a saved event id *in the URL*. But a **new event in the editor has no id in the URL**, so `!urlEid()` is `true` — and `snap.eid` is non-empty (a stale `clickedEid` from a previously viewed event), so it fires `NAVIGATE_TO_EVENT`. That's a full tab navigation → "Leave site?" → your unsaved event is gone.

The original intent ("unless they're already on the edit screen") should key on whether we're **on an `/eventedit` page at all** — not on whether the URL has an id. On the editor (new *or* existing), navigating always reloads and drops edits, so it must be skipped.

## Why the reload happened

The click handler tries to take you to the event's edit screen, *unless you're already there*. But "already there" was tested with `!urlEid()` — i.e. "is there no event id in the URL?" A **brand-new, unsaved event has no id in its `/eventedit` URL**, so that check read as "not on the edit screen" and fired `NAVIGATE_TO_EVENT`. Background then did a `chrome.tabs.update(... url ...)` = a full tab navigation → the editor's `beforeunload` guard shows **"Leave site? Changes you made may not be saved"** → your in-progress event is discarded on reload. (The navigation target was a stale `clickedEid`, compounding it.)

## Fix

The guard now keys on whether you're on an `/eventedit` page at all, not on whether the URL has an id:

```js
const onEditPage = /\/eventedit/.test(location.pathname);
if (snap.eid && !onEditPage) { …navigate… }
```

So:
- **Creating/editing in the full editor** (new or existing) → never navigates → no reload, no "Leave site?", nothing lost. The side panel still opens via `openFor`/`followFor` (which use messaging + storage, not navigation).
- **From a detail popover** (not an `/eventedit` page) → still navigates you to the edit screen for that existing event, as before.

Reload the extension + Calendar tab and retry: create a new event, click Manage Visitors — the panel should open with no page reload and no leave-site prompt.

One adjacent thing I noticed but did **not** change: on the new-event editor the panel opens against whatever `snap.eid` is (often empty → it shows the "save the event to issue passes" notice, which is correct; but a stale `clickedEid` could in theory point it at the wrong event). If you ever see the panel open to the *wrong* event from a new-event editor, tell me and I'll tighten how `clickedEid` is cleared — it's a separate, smaller issue from the reload.

> DEVELOPER

but we would be picking the temporary event id from the system right, like the one we checked if the temporary id becomes the actual id, right?

> AGENT

Right — and this is the key nuance that makes skipping the navigation safe rather than lossy. Let me separate the two things, because "we have the right id" and "we can act on it" aren't the same here.

## The id itself: yes, it's the stable one
When you open the **full editor** for a new event, Google pre-allocates the event id and puts it in the `/r/eventedit/<eid>` URL. That decodes to `<eventId> <calendarId>` exactly like the saved ones we decoded earlier (`786oqbqr… junaid@appointy.com`), and Google keeps that same `eventId` when you hit Save — the "temporary" id *is* the eventual real id. Our `readSurface()` reads it via `urlEid()`, so the snapshot's `eid` for a new-event editor is that durable id, not a throwaway. So your instinct is correct: we're picking up the id that becomes the actual one.

## But we still can't resolve it until it's saved
Having the right id ≠ being able to issue passes. The engine resolves an event by its canonical **`iCalUID`** via the Calendar API (`events.get`). An unsaved event has **no server record and no `iCalUID` yet** — `events.get` would 404 — which is why the panel shows "Save the event to issue passes" for a new event. Once you save:
- the same id now exists on the server (with an `iCalUID`),
- the background sync + auto-follow (`FOLLOW_EVENT`) pick it up by that id, and the panel resolves normally.

So nothing is lost by *not* navigating: we're already on that event's editor, the panel is already pointed at it, and the moment you save, it becomes resolvable under the same id. Navigating would have re-loaded the tab (even to "the same" event, since the URL encoding differs — `primary` vs your email, padding, etc.) and discarded the unsaved form — all cost, zero benefit.

## One caveat worth flagging
This clean story holds **when the new-event URL actually carries the eid** (the full editor). If you start from an entry point where the URL has *no* eid (e.g. the small quick-create popover), `readSurface()` falls back to `clickedEid`, which could be stale from a previously viewed event — pointing the panel at the wrong event. In practice the `MutationObserver` usually clears `clickedEid` in that state (so `snap.eid` becomes `''` → "unsaved" notice, which is correct-ish), but it's not airtight.

If you'd like, I can harden that: on an `/eventedit` page with no `urlEid()`, force `snap.eid = ''` (treat as a genuinely-new unsaved event) instead of ever using a stale `clickedEid`. Want me to do that?

## Change

In `readSurface()`, the full-screen editor branch now returns `eid: ''` instead of `clickedEid ?? ''`:

```js
if (/\/eventedit/.test(location.pathname)) {
  return { el: dialog ?? document.body, eid: '' };
}
```

**Why this is correct:** an existing event always carries its eid in the `/eventedit/<eid>` URL, which is handled by the `if (fromUrl)` branch above. So the only way execution reaches this branch is a **brand-new, unsaved event** (no eid in the URL yet). In that state the truthful answer is "no saved id" → `''`, which makes the panel show the "Save the event to issue passes" notice. Falling back to `clickedEid` could surface a stale id from a previously viewed event and point the panel at the wrong one — exactly the airtightness gap I flagged.

The detail/quick-create popover branch still uses `clickedEid` (correct there — clicking the event chip sets it for that popover), and `clickedEid` remains used by the click handler and the observer, so nothing else is affected.

Net behavior on the new-event editor: the snapshot reads as unsaved (`eid: ''`), the panel opens to the "save first" state, no navigation/reload, and once you save, the sync + auto-follow resolve it under its real id. Reload the extension + Calendar tab to pick it up.

> AGENT

Type-check and build pass.

## What was causing the intermittency

Injection only happened when `render()` ran (on clicks, popstate, or a `MutationObserver` childList change). But a surface's **layout can settle without a DOM mutation** — Google renders the rows, then a later layout pass gives them their width. Our anchor finders (`findContentList`, `firstIconLedRow`, `rowInset`) rely on `getBoundingClientRect().width`, so if `findAnchor` ran a beat too early (widths still 0, width-filter excludes the rows), it returned no anchor — and with no further childList mutation, `render()` never fired again, so we never retried. Whether the timing worked out was basically luck → "sometimes renders, sometimes doesn't."

## Fix

`update()` now runs a **bounded retry loop**: it tries to inject, and if the anchor isn't ready yet, it retries every 150ms for ~4.5s (30 attempts), stopping the instant injection succeeds. This guarantees we catch the moment Google finishes rendering/laying out the surface, independent of whether a mutation fires.

I also made the floating fallback show only after a **~450ms grace period** (`attempts >= 3`) rather than immediately — so on a normally-rendering surface the native in-surface button just appears, with no flash of the FAB getting swapped out a moment later. The FAB still appears as a genuine fallback if injection truly can't find an anchor.

`clearTimeout(retryTimer)` at the top of `update()` ensures overlapping calls (observer + retry) don't stack timers, and `update(false)` cancels any pending retry when the surface closes.

Reload the extension + Calendar tab and exercise it a few times — open/close the modal and the edit screen repeatedly; the button should now appear reliably each time rather than intermittently.

## Root cause

When I added the safe wrappers earlier, I converted every `chrome.*` call **except one**: `maybeFollow()` still called `chrome.runtime.sendMessage(...).catch(...)` directly. That function runs from `render()` whenever the panel is open and you're viewing a saved event — and `sendMessage` **throws synchronously** the moment the context is invalidated (an extension reload), which the `.catch()` can't intercept → uncaught error. The `render()` guard normally stops this, but there's a window where the context dies between the guard check and the `sendMessage` call (and the error context — `?msg=Could+not+find+the+requested+event` — is exactly the kind of navigation churn that races with a reload).

## Fix

`maybeFollow()` now uses `safeSend()` like every other call site. I verified with grep that the **only** remaining `chrome.runtime.sendMessage` / `chrome.storage.session.*` references are inside the three guarded wrappers (`safeSend`, `safeStorageGet`, `safeStorageSet`), plus `chrome.runtime.onMessage.addListener` (registration only — never throws) and `extAlive()`'s `chrome.runtime?.id` check (wrapped in try/catch). So there are no unguarded chrome API calls left in the content script.

Reload the extension + Calendar tab. After the next extension reload, the orphaned content script should go silent instead of throwing.

> AGENT

Good instinct — I'd push for it, and actually go a step further. Let me give you the reasoning, then implement it.

## My take: yes, remove the auto-navigation

The auto-nav is a **tab hijack with no functional payoff**:

1. **The panel doesn't need it.** The panel resolves the event itself via the Calendar API (`events.get` → `iCalUID`). The edit screen being open contributes nothing to issuing passes. As you say, there's no realtime calendar-edit sync that would justify it.
2. **It fights our constraints.** It's a full page reload — slow on mid/low-end Android and patchy warehouse Wi-Fi, exactly the conditions we optimize for. Spending a reload for zero function is the wrong trade.
3. **It's surprising.** The user clicked "Manage Visitors" expecting a panel, not for their whole calendar view to jump and reload. "Don't hijack the user" is one of our house rules.
4. **It's been a bug factory.** The new-event "Leave site?" data loss, the cross-serving `primary`-vs-email URL bug, the list-item-opening-edit-screen complaint — all downstream of auto-nav.

One refinement on your proposal: make it **one** explicit "Open in Calendar" control living in the panel's event summary (low-prominence, not a big CTA — registering visitors stays the primary action). Then *every* entry point (in-surface button, nudge, list pick) just opens the panel, and the single panel button is the one predictable way to jump to the event. That's more consistent than scattering nav across triggers.

So I'll: (1) drop the `NAVIGATE_TO_EVENT` call from the in-surface button, the nudge "Manage", and the panel's list-pick; (2) add an "Open in Calendar" button to the panel summary.

  "new_string": "      if (panelOpen || opening) {\n        followFor(snap);\n      } else {\n        opening = true;\n        // Clear the latch if the panel never connects (open blocked/failed).\n        setTimeout(() => {\n          opening = false;\n        }, 3000);\n        openFor(snap); // opens the panel + sets the active event\n      }\n      // Note: we intentionally do NOT navigate the tab to the event here. The\n      // panel resolves the event itself (Calendar API), so reloading the page\n      // adds nothing but cost + surprise. \"Open in Calendar\" in the panel is the\n      // explicit, user-initiated way to jump to the event.\n    });"
}

> AGENT

Done — type-check and build pass.

## What changed

**Auto-navigation removed from every trigger:**
- **In-surface "Manage Visitors" button** (`calendar.content.ts`) — no longer sends `NAVIGATE_TO_EVENT`; it just opens/points the panel.
- **Nudge "Manage" button** — same; the button now re-enables off the `OPEN_FOR_EVENT` call instead of the (removed) navigate.
- **Panel's event-list pick** (`VisitPanel.tsx` `onPick`) — just sets the active event; no tab navigation. (This was also your earlier complaint — clicking a list item opened the edit screen.)

**One explicit control added** — an "Open in Calendar" text button in the panel's event summary (`ExternalLink` icon, low-prominence, left-aligned). It sends `NAVIGATE_TO_EVENT` with the active `eid`, so jumping to the event is now a single, predictable, user-initiated action. The background `NAVIGATE_TO_EVENT` handler stays (still used by this button, with its `primary`→email eid fix intact).

## Resulting behavior
- Clicking Manage Visitors (anywhere) opens the panel and nothing reloads — no tab hijack, no "Leave site?", no FAB-flash-during-load, none of the navigation-driven bugs.
- The panel resolves and follows the event on its own (Calendar API + auto-follow), so removing the reload costs no function.
- When the user genuinely wants the event on screen, "Open in Calendar" takes them there.

One thing I left as-is: the panel's top "Following the event open in Calendar" hint — that's the *auto-follow* indicator (the panel still tracks whatever event you open in Calendar while it's docked), which is independent of the navigation we removed, so it's still accurate. If you'd rather reword or drop it now that we're de-emphasizing the calendar-tab coupling, say so.

A note for your stakeholder framing: this is purely a client UX change in the extension — no engine, cost, or scope impact. It *removes* behavior (and a class of bugs), so it's low-risk to ship.

Reload the extension + Calendar tab to verify: Manage Visitors opens the panel with no reload; "Open in Calendar" in the panel jumps to the event.

the open in calendar button is not properly designed

> AGENT

Yeah — I zeroed its horizontal padding, which kills the text button's hit area and state layer and makes it read as cramped raw text. The correct MD3 idiom for a text button aligned to a container edge is to **keep** its padding and pull it left with a negative margin equal to that padding, so the *label* aligns to the edge while the touch target and hover state stay intact.

> AGENT

Type-check and build pass.

## Fix

The problem was `paddingLeft: 0, paddingRight: 0` on the text button — that strips its 12px horizontal padding, so the touch target shrinks to the text bounds and the hover state-layer hugs the label, making it look like cramped raw text rather than a button.

I replaced it with `marginLeft: calc(-1 * var(--space-md))` (−12px = the text button's own padding). This is the standard MD3 text-button placement: the **label** lines up flush with the card's content edge, while the padding, hit area, and hover/press state layer all stay intact. So it now reads as a proper, tappable text button that's visually aligned with the title/metadata above it.

Reload and check the panel — the "Open in Calendar" button should look like an intentional secondary action (primary-colored label + external-link icon, proper hover) sitting just under the event details, aligned to the card edge.

If by "not properly designed" you meant the **placement** rather than the button styling (e.g. you'd prefer it as an icon action next to the title, or as a footer action at the bottom of the card), tell me which and I'll move it — that's a quick change.

> AGENT

I've now traced the most likely mechanism. Two things conspire:

1. **The MutationObserver is debounced at 150ms.** During a continuous scroll, Google fires DOM mutations faster than that (lazy rendering, hovercards), so the debounce timer keeps resetting and `render()` **never fires while you're actively scrolling**. If Google re-renders the modal body and detaches our appended button in that window, nothing re-injects it until scrolling stops — and you perceive it as "disappeared."
2. **Orphaned content script.** If the extension was reloaded without reloading the tab, the context is dead, `teardown()` disconnects the observer, and once Google removes the button it can never come back.

I'll harden against (1) with a periodic safety re-assert, and make (2) clean up after itself instead of leaving a dead button.

## What the bug is

Two things, and I hardened both:

**1. Debounce starvation during scroll (the likely live-context cause).** The `MutationObserver` that drives re-injection is debounced at 150ms. A continuous scroll makes Google fire DOM mutations *faster* than 150ms (lazy rendering, hovercards), so the debounce timer keeps resetting and `render()` never actually runs while you're scrolling. If Google re-renders the modal body and detaches our appended button in that window, there's nothing to put it back until you stop scrolling — so it "disappears for a few seconds."

**Fix:** the existing 1s `editorPoll` now also calls `render()` each tick. That's a debounce-independent safety net — even if the observer is starved, the button is re-asserted (re-injected if missing) within ~1s.

**2. Orphaned content script (if you reloaded the extension without reloading the tab).** Once the context is invalidated, `teardown()` disconnects the observer, so after Google removes the button on scroll it can never come back — and previously it left a *dead* button that looks clickable but does nothing.

**Fix:** `teardown()` now also removes the button (`button.update(false)`), so an orphaned script cleans up instead of leaving a zombie. The real remedy for this case is reloading the Calendar tab to get a fresh, live content script.

## So, which is it for you?
- If it comes back after you stop scrolling → that was the debounce starvation, now covered by the 1s re-assert.
- If the button just vanishes and never returns even when idle → that's the orphaned script; **reload the Calendar tab** after reloading the extension.

Reload the extension **and the Calendar tab**, then open a modal and scroll continuously — the button should now persist (or re-appear within ~1s) rather than dropping out.

If it still vanishes on a freshly-reloaded tab, the next best diagnostic is to paste the modal's scroll container DOM (the element that actually scrolls) — Google may be virtualizing/recycling the rows, in which case I'd anchor our button outside the recycled region instead of inside the rows list.
